#7300. How Bustle Digital Group is helping Pampers and Innisfree show up in AI recommendations

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

Bustle Digital Group is using Profound’s AI measurement tools to help brands, including Pampers and Innisfree, show up in AI-generated recommendations.

The publisher’s GEO offering, called ECHO internally, uses Profound analytics and AI agents trained on content from BDG’s titles to generate creative briefs and guidelines. BDG’s creative team uses those materials to produce content designed to be cited by AI platforms. At least three brands have paid for the offering.

An uptick in client inquiries this year prompted BDG to develop the offering, said Graham Howes, svp of distribution and AI intelligence at BDG, which owns titles like Bustle, Nylon, Elite Daily and Scary Mommy.

BDG is the only publisher currently working with Profound to turn this process into an advertiser offering.

In one campaign, which ran between Aug 11–Sep 30, Korean beauty brand Innisfree’s visibility across 200 tracked skincare prompts grew 30%, while Bustle’s citation rank rose from #709 to #12, according to a BDG case study based on Profound data.

“By combining trusted editorial environments, answer engine optimization, and prompt-level measurement, we were able to demonstrate that AI visibility can be measured, optimized, and ultimately transformed into a meaningful competitive advantage,” said Julien Bouzitat, gm of Innisfree U.S., part of the Amore Pacific Group.

Baby care brand Pampers moved from #2 to #1 in AI visibility for fragrance-free diaper questions following a Scary Mommy explainer addressing Swaddlers’ fragrance-free switch. The explainer ran between Sep 4–17.

“There isn’t a defined playbook for how publishers should be approaching this. So we really had to come at this from a very DIY place of first understanding the space,” Howes said.

The process begins with an RFP and conversations with the brand on their AI visibility goals, Howes explained. Then comes a two- to four-week baseline research phase, where BDG and Profound analyze eight to 10 topics that the advertiser wants to increase its visibility against within an AI answer engine, and 25 to 50 prompts [on each topic] to understand the brand’s competitive standing and what sites the answer engines are currently citing.

BDG then uses its own AI agents, trained on its titles’ content, to develop an editorial strategy for the branded content. This includes potential article topics, headline structure, and tone, Howes explained. That strategy is then presented to the advertiser.

“The advertiser has no hand in the actual creative development at all. We leave that entirely up to our editors, partly because we want to make sure that we are following all of the best practices of what gets cited, and something that is very heavy-handed on the advertorial front is very unlikely to do that,” Howes said.

AI agents within Profound then design the creative briefs, which get handed off to BDG’s creative team. They write in their own voice, but follow guidelines set by the agents, such as including an FAQ section at the bottom of the article, or the type of article format, such as a listicle or an explainer.

“You’ve got to be able to connect analytics into action,” said Josh Blyska, head of insights at Profound.

Once the content is live, it’s cross-promoted on Reddit, where BDG is running paid media. This helps build awareness among relevant communities of Reddit users, which “still matters a lot in the overall algorithm of what is being picked up and why,” Howes said. Reddit, he added, remains a significant citation source for answer engines.”

BDG tracks the performance of those posts throughout the campaign, he added.

This is the big question, especially given attribution is difficult because AI platforms offer limited insight into how people use their answers to make purchasing decisions, making it hard for publishers to attribute AI’s influence on traffic, engagement and conversions.

BDG tracks the performance of the GEO-branded content by analyzing metrics including the brand’s visibility on AI platforms, and BDG’s citation share and citation rank, mainly across ChatGPT, Gemini and Google’s AI Overviews.

Profound measures AI visibility by whether the brand appears in an AI response. Citation share measures the percentage of total citations in a given set of AI-generated answers that come from a particular publisher’s site, Blyska said. (Citation share is typically between 0.1% and 3%, he noted.) Citation rank measures how a publisher’s content ranks against competitors based on how frequently each is cited in AI-generated answers.

“We want to really be able to prove to the best of our ability, that these two goals are interconnected when we run programs in this kind of way,” Howes said.

Using Profound, BDG identifies examples of its content appearing in AI answers, monitors which articles generate the most citations and makes real-time optimizations to improve performance, Howes said. Advertisers then receive a report detailing the results.

“Now the question is, does the influence layer – i.e., ChatGPT, where people find the conviction to decide what to buy – net cleanly into the transaction layer, which is often through Google into the website? And the answer is no, not right now,” Blyska said.

Howes declined to share how much revenue its GEO offering was generating, or how much BDG was charging brands for this offering. It’s sold as a standalone offering, or bundled into existing custom content packages. Profound does not receive a cut of these deals.

“We see this more as an extension of [our] custom content opportunities, not a replacement to them,” Howes said. “Clients can still have those opportunities to reach our audiences on our sites, on our social channels, but also pair that with these types of opportunities to also reach similar audiences that are now choosing to engage an answer engine to have a question answered.”

Ashish Jacob, director of GEO at Go Fish Digital, said consistent measurement matters more than which analytics platforms a company uses to track visibility.

“The most valuable metrics include how often a brand appears in relevant AI responses, which sources are cited, sentiment, competitive share of voice, and how those results change over time. It’s also important to understand the prompts, AI platforms, and sampling methods behind the data, since different tools may produce different results. First-party reporting is becoming increasingly valuable as well,” Jacob said.

Profound is seeing some renewals for 2027, as well as exploring running always-on, yearlong campaigns, Howes said.

“A lot of advertisers realize that this content is very powerful, but it also has a half-life to it, and if you want to really make sure that you have an ongoing presence – especially when we think about our partners in beauty and fashion, where so much of what they’re doing is very seasonal – that they need to really have a more always-on approach to their content strategy,” Howes said.

These GEO offerings require publishers to be nimble, Howes said. Because AI answer engines are constantly changing their algorithms, GEO requires ongoing monitoring, research and content optimization, he said. Publishers must continually adapt their strategies to keep brands visible in AI-generated answers. But that’s an opportunity in itself, to act “not just as content partners” but as “a knowledge resource” to help brands understand what’s happening within AI answer engine recommendations, he added.

Howes sees GEO as an opportunity not just to improve its own visibility in AI search, but to grow its advertising business. Branded and custom content is BDG’s largest revenue source, he said. Measuring and demonstrating how GEO efforts benefit advertisers will be critical to strengthening brand partnerships and expanding these offerings as a revenue stream, Howes noted.

“I really want to better understand how this new paradigm we find ourselves in really plays a direct role in our business, not just our own editorial growth. To be able to have that kind of data and visibility, not just in our own efforts, but in our advertisers’ efforts as well, is fundamental to what we do because we need to be able to have that level of transparency to grow the kind of partnerships that we’re looking to grow into next year with these types of packages,” Howes said.

More brands are gravitating toward live shopping, a growing channel that is set to play an outsized influence on shopping trends.

Twitch is swinging big with a new short-form content push that should help more creators get discovered on-platform.

Yahoo is the first licensee of the Internet Licensing Collective, offering publishers a new way to get paid for content used in AI search.

#7298. How CeraVe’s ‘Dune’ deal takes the brand’s social-first playbook global

The L’Oréal brand’s first film partnership makes it the “Official Moisturizer of Arrakis” ahead of the blockbuster’s Dec. 18 release.

CeraVe last week partnered with “Dune: Part Three” to become the “Official Moisturizer of Arrakis,” the desert planet that serves as the sci-fi epic’s titular setting. The tie-up with Warner Bros. Pictures and Legendary Pictures represents the L'Oréal brand’s first film partnership, with a campaign that will roll out ahead of the movie’s Dec. 18 theatrical release.

The brand on Monday revealed the next chapter in the campaign. A humorous “Sandworm Wrangler” video, shot in the style of behind-the-scenes content, shows how a wrangler of one of the film’s iconic sandworms uses CeraVe Moisturizing Cream — which now offers 120 hours of hydration — on the dry-skinned creature.

The debut “Precious Resource” spot reimagined the lore of Arrakis, an arid planet where every drop of moisture is preserved, replacing the story’s central resource, spice, with CeraVe. The “Dune” partnership is the latest iteration of a “medutainment” strategy that combines science with entertainment and has helped grow CeraVe into a $2 billion brand.

“When it comes to the playbook, that's something we are reproducing here with ‘Dune,’” said Vincent Chauvière, global brand president at CeraVe. “It's a multipoint campaign that is social-first, starting with a bit of excitement and surprise, and then we are going full media.”

Along with co-branded content, the campaign spans paid, earned, social, digital, retail, creator and experiential activations. The brand will also launch out-of-home ads, creator gifting and corporate screening experiences, as well as sponsor film premiere events in Los Angeles and the U.K.

“Our goal is to create partnerships that extend the worlds of our films in ways that feel unexpected, culturally relevant and genuinely entertaining,” Julie Moore, executive vice president, global brand partnerships at Warner Bros. Pictures, said in a statement.

The “Dune” campaign was created and produced by OBB Bolded, OBB’s branded content studio, with content filmed in Samalayuca Dunes in Mexico and Mexico City and directed by Maca Rubio from Stink Films. OBB was also behind an award-winning “Moisturize Like a Derm” effort that appointed NBA star Kevin Durant as the brand’s “Face of Legs.”

“It's been a campaign that has been working at bringing awareness to the brand, but also to the usage of a body moisturizer, bringing our science to new consumers, particularly men. We've been recruiting more men this year, thanks to this partnership [with the NBA],” Chauvière explained.

The “Dune” partnership is rolling out globally, and the campaign is designed to pair content around the billion-dollar franchise with market-specific dermatologists and experiential activations. CeraVe is growing at a double-digit rate this year and gaining share around the world, and geographic expansion is key to the brand’s future growth, Chauvière said.

“The partnership with ‘Dune’ [is] a way to have a more consistent brand tone of voice and point of view. We have local teams that have a lot of local activations, and the influencer world is a local world. So it's all all about the balance,” the executive said.

The local push builds on CeraVe’s larger creator strategy, which helps the brand reach more than 15 million followers across platforms. The brand this summer united over 100 creators from 22 countries for its first Global CerAwards in Los Angeles. The event included CeraVe Studios, an immersive content creation experience hosted on a Hollywood studio lot, that generated nearly 60 million views of new content, per Chauvière.

“It was a moment of visibility, but it's also a moment to gather all the local influencers together and reunite around this idea of bringing creativity in the beauty world and making science accessible and entertaining at the same time,” the executive said.

#7295. Substack job listing hints at a bigger advertising push in 2027

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

Few took Substack’s co-founder and chief writing officer Hamish McKenzie literally when he told Digiday in 2025 that ads were a long way off. A new job hire suggests they should have.

The online publishing and newsletter platform is currently hiring a U.S.-based product manager of payments to help expand monetization on Substack and find better ways to support paying creators, according to the company’s careers page.

The job spec states that the scope of the role includes gifting, retention offers and improvements to the payments and subscription experience, alongside opportunities to explore commerce, and (crucially) advertising-related monetization over time. The role, which comes with a cash compensation package between $210,000 and $230,000, will also help shape future ad opportunities as the business evolves.

Substack first signalled a willingness to reconsider ads in March 2025 when Digiday spoke to McKenzie. He described ads as an “interesting business” for Substack, while suggesting it was “some way off in the distant future.”

Since making those comments, Substack has gradually started taking a more active role in connecting brands with publishers, although its efforts so far have focused on sponsorships rather than conventional ads.

Historically, ads on Substack have been creator-led rather than platform-led. Brands typically partnered directly with individual newsletters or creator networks, arranging sponsorships across newsletters and podcasts.

In June, it announced the first phase of a native sponsorship program, introducing Creator Kits to help publishers showcase their audiences and attract brand partners. In October, it introduced three sponsorship formats:

But the latest job spec suggests Substack could be considering a broader role for advertising in its monetization strategy from next year.

For some, this brings both opportunities and risks. Substack’s potential pivot toward paid advertising feels like “the end of an idealistic era,” according to Brenda Imeson, director of strategy at Brave Bison. Another platform that built its identity partly around resisting ads is now facing the commercial realities of sustaining its business.

“Subscription fatigue is biting, paywalls inevitably gatekeep good journalism and limit reach. If handled sensitively, native sponsorships will allow writers to keep more work free without hollowing out editorial integrity and could democratize access to great writing,” Imeson said. “The test will be if Substack can take brand money and keep thoughtful discourse from degrading into attention grabbing algorithmic bait.”

CEOs have never cared much for holdcos. Enterprise solutions, on the other hand, get a meeting.

#7294. Amazon puts Arnold Schwarzenegger in Santa suit to extend holiday push

A new spot ties into the upcoming movie “The Man with the Bag,” showing Amazon again tapping into its own programming to promote its range of offerings.

Amazon is once again tapping into its Prime ecosystem to integrate one of its entertainment properties into an advertising campaign. Taking inspiration from “The Man with the Bag,” which premiers Dec. 2, the e-commerce platform has created a light-hearted holiday ad featuring a familiar face.

“Santa Schwarzenegger” follows the actor as he uses a Santa suit, delivered by Prime, to explore New York City unnoticed. During his adventure, Schwarzenegger is able to watch a game with bar patrons, catch a holiday movie and use his strength to help a family get a Christmas tree on the roof of their car. It ends with the actor using an Amazon Locker to pick up a pair of skates so he can enjoy time on an ice rink. The minute-long spot is set to “Make Someone Happy” by Jimmy Durante.

Schwarzenegger will also appear as Santa throughout the season outside of the spot and movie, per press details.

Amazon has made finding moments of joy a central part of its holiday strategy. “Santa Schwarzenegger” follows the longer “Joy Delivered” spot, which tells the story of a young woman going on a “Planes, Trains and Automobiles”-style journey to make it home to her family in time for the holidays, giving away the gifts she bought along the way.

This is not the first time Amazon has drawn inspiration from a Prime Video title in its advertising. In May, Amazon’s Brand Innovation Lab released a spot with Liquid I.V. featuring the leading actors from its hockey romance show, “Off Campus,” marking the first time Prime Video leads appeared in a co-branded spot.

“Santa Schwarzenegger” allows the e-commerce giant to promote its delivery services while also showing how its properties can be used for advertising.

Amazon’s ad revenue hit $19.6 billion in Q3, a 12% year-over-year increase, per an earnings statement. However, this is a significant dip from the 26% YoY growth rate seen just a quarter earlier, leading to a stock dip for the company.

#7293. How brands are banking on live shopping this holiday season

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

The story was first published by Digiday sibling Glossy

Many brands have accepted that, to effectively compete this holiday season, last year’s marketing playbook won’t cut it.

More brands are gravitating toward live shopping, a growing channel that is set to play an outsized influence on shopping trends. According to data from eMarketer, livestream retail sales in the U.S. are set to reach nearly $20 billion this year. And annual spend per buyer on livestreams is growing by 23% to an average of $339.27.

Brands like Tarte Cosmetics, Crocs, Beachwaver and Gap are planning to make use of live shopping, particularly on TikTok, this holiday season. Tarte and Crocs plan to launch exclusive products just for TikTok live shopping, with Crocs’ exclusives including a Disney collaboration. TikTok has seen livestream shopping sales double in the first half of this year, and use of the hashtag #holidayshopping rose 263% year over year in October.

Ziad Bon, founder of Oudware, the 3-year-old fragrance retailer that sells imported fragrances from the Middle East, told Glossy that live shopping has become an integral part of his company’s holiday strategy. Oudware already has a fairly comprehensive strategy, hosting live shopping shows on weekdays for its 215,000 followers. From Black Friday through mid-December, Oudware will be increasing its live frequency, going live on TikTok Shop every day from 2 p.m. to 7 p.m. EST. Oudware already makes more than $200,000 per month from live shopping on average and does more than $20 million in annual revenue. Its products sell for around $30-$100 per bottle.

Bon said it’s important for brands engaging in live shopping to have a plan for each live. Oudware’s live strategy is to spend the first hour of each live with what Bon called the “gift hotline,” where the team takes feedback and requests from viewers on which items and fragrances they want to see go on sale that evening. The next few hours are spent showcasing products and putting them up for sale through the regular TikTok Shop sales channel. The last hour or two of each live are reserved for auctions, a feature on TikTok Shop announced in 2025 and rolled out more broadly this year.

For Oudware, which sells about 3,000 products per day on TikTok Shop livestreams, saving the auctions for last is crucial. Live shopping conversion rates tend to be much higher than standard e-commerce, and consumers are increasingly drawn to auctions due to the possibility of lower prices. Adobe data from this year found that people are nearly 70% more likely to buy something during a live shopping stream if they see others bidding as well.

“Personally, I hate auctions,” Bon said. “A lot of our fragrance manufacturers don’t want their fragrances sold at auction, and the prices are often super low. But as a seller, it’s really hard to get engagement if you’re not doing auctions, since that’s what the customers want.”

Running auctions only at the end of the live has two purposes: It lets Oudware spend the majority of a livestream selling products at their regular prices and saves the auctions for when the live has the most viewers. More viewers means more bids, which means Oudware can sell products at auction for closer to the normal asking price.

Live shopping was a big success for brands last holiday season. TikTok Shop saw over $500 million in sales throughout the holiday period in 2025. Meanwhile, on Black Friday last year, Whatnot saw $75 million in sales in one day, tripling its previous peak from the year before.

TikTok itself is also introducing new features to support live shopping for the holidays. In the past year, big name brands and retailers like Fenty Beauty and Sephora have joined TikTok Shop. Products sold on a live will now have a visible badge if they’re part of TikTok’s Fulfilled by TikTok program (similar to Amazon Prime). Bon said the ability to ship product right after a live ends and have it arrive three days later is likely to be a big draw for customers. And, starting this fall, TikTok Shop will be working with Happy Returns to let shoppers return purchased items to any of Happy Returns’ 10,000 drop-off locations across the U.S.

“Live shopping has become one of the most engaging formats on TikTok Shop, and we expect even more brands to embrace it this holiday season as they look for new ways to connect with shoppers,” said Patrick Nommensen, president of strategic initiatives at TikTok Shop, in an email to Glossy. “What makes Live particularly compelling during the holidays is its ability to turn product discovery into an interactive shopping experience, allowing consumers to watch demonstrations, ask questions, and hear directly from brands and creators before making a purchase.”

Bustle Digital Group has teamed up with Profound to help brands like Pampers and Innisfree boost their visibility in AI recommendations in a new GEO-offering.

Twitch is swinging big with a new short-form content push that should help more creators get discovered on-platform.

Yahoo is the first licensee of the Internet Licensing Collective, offering publishers a new way to get paid for content used in AI search.

#7288. Media Buying Briefing: Buyers grapple with incoming Amazon reporting changes

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

This Media Buying Briefing covers the latest in agency news and media buying for Digiday+ members and is distributed over email every Monday at 10 a.m. ET. More from the series →

Changes in the way that key media platforms measure impressions mean that performance and pricing benchmarks may be thrown into disarray come the new year.

Amazon has estimated brands will see 63% fewer impressions for display ads appearing across its retail properties, including its homepage, product pages and search results, according to a notice distributed among advertisers seen by Digiday. It’s due to the platform’s decision to shift from counting impressions the moment they begin downloading to a user’s device, to when they actually render on the user’s screen, from Jan. 1. Google announced a similar transition at the beginning of September, effective Feb. 17; spokespeople from both tech companies declined to comment.

In turn, six buyers who spoke to Digiday said they’re working to amend reporting methodologies and soothe client nerves.

Buyers fear that a reduction in reported impressions could mean that supply is constrained, driving up CPMs. “If there’s going to be fewer impressions, your CPMs will be higher,” explained Hannah Schatz, vp of programmatic operations at Coegi.

At the same time, the stricter impression reporting methodology means that lower-impact display placements further down Store pages will become even less desirable for advertisers — while top-of-page slots that stand a greater chance of influencing shoppers will see an increase in demand.

“There’s going to be probably more competition on the platform,” said Nola Ladd, awareness media supervisor at media agency Collective Measures.

Amazon has not issued guidance regarding the expected impact on pricing or costs, or whether it might update its billing methodology around the new impression reporting, according to Stagwell Media Platform’s global product managing director, Dru Sil.

“The implications are up to us to discover,” he said.

While the drop in impressions might unnerve some clients, it’s unlikely to provoke advertisers to reduce spending. Sil suggested that most advertisers will maintain spending levels, in order to establish new performance benchmarks in January and February.

“I don’t expect clients to like dramatically scale up budgets to try to get ahead of the curve. You’re likely going to continue to run what you’ve been running because you have an expected outcome that you’re trying to obtain from Amazon, regardless of the specific ad format,” said Sil.

He added: “You need to build that baseline anyway, right? And the only way to build that baseline is to be running ads.”

Benchmarking may be the real problem buyers face when they shake off the new year’s hangover.

Automated reporting processes, or media mix models that use impressions as a key input, might need to be recalibrated to account for the expected drop in reported impressions. “If you have a lot of automated processes, optimizations, reconciliations, built around these data sets already, you’re going to have to do a little bit of work,” said Paul DeJarnatt, vp and head of investment at Novus.

For most buyers, Amazon’s and Google’s tinkering with impression reporting merely underlines the importance of focusing on outcomes, rather than media KPIs, to understand campaign performance.

“The bottom line is, we don’t expect this to change campaign results. The shift will make reporting metrics look different, but it shouldn’t move ROAS or CPA, which are the outcomes our clients care about,” said Jared Smith, senior director of strategy and innovation, January Digital.

“Impressions, CPMs, they shouldn’t be north star metrics,” said Ernie DeColte, svp of media at indie agency Level. “They’re tool metrics we use to optimize towards business impact. This might be a wake-up call to a few advertisers. Maybe it’s time to think about buying differently.”

Still, communicating this change to clients who might be alarmed when they see impression counts fall off a cliff (or CPMs spike) is something media buyers across the industry are attempting to get ahead of.

“The client, even though that metric is misleading, is used to seeing a big number,” said Ladd.

“They’re used to seeing really low CPMs. They’re used to seeing really great impression volume, and that change and that difference is going to require agencies and media experts to really think about the way that they present success to the client,” she added.

Just because it’s got a donut graph doesn’t mean a report is saying very much. According to an Experian survey of 400 marketers, the profusion of dashboards and data isn’t helping marketers back up their decisions.

“In the past, a company like Unilever, buying big media in Brazil from TV Globo, had a significant competitive advantage. Now you are buying from Meta or from Google or from Amazon or from Walmart or from TikTok, and that model doesn’t favor a scale of purchase. It favors the quality of engagement you generate on the platform.”

—Unilever CEO Fernando Fernandez, speaking during its Capital Markets Day last week, on why better creative could be the secret to making its marketing operation work harder.

Local sports rights have gotten more complex and confusing as the regional sports network business changed. The leagues are now looking to simplify that offering.

As AI automates advertising workflows, the contest is shifting to who can make the decisions that deliver results.

#7272. Senior Forward Deployed Engineer at Clutch

Clutch builds AI agents that work on behalf of credit unions. Emma, our collections agent, is live at five credit unions and handled 60,000+ member conversations in her first twelve weeks at one of them. Behind her is a growing portfolio: origination, fraud step-up, member (re)engagement and activation agents, all built on the same Agents Platform (TypeScript, NestJS, our own agent SDK, MCP, Temporal, promptfoo).

As a FDE you are one of the two engineers on an agent. You take it from "sold" to "live and performing" at a specific credit union: you sit in the discovery sessions, you build the integrations to their core banking and loan systems, you author and tune the agent's behavior, you run the live tests with their staff, and you stay with it through go-live and the first weeks of production. When something needs a person in the room, you are the one who flies.

This is a senior engineering role, not a solutions or support role. You write production code in our monorepo every week: integrations, modules, and the agents themselves (prompts, tools, evals), together with the other engineers on the pod. The difference from a traditional engineer is that you are on site more often, you make judgment calls in front of the customer, and you own the outcome. What you learn in the field flows straight back into the product: defaults, playbooks, and the platform capabilities the next agent will need.

You build; our AI Operations Managers deliver. The pod (pod lead plus engineers) designs and builds each agent in a high-touch way with the first customers. Once the agent is defined and live, AI Ops coordinates the implementations, configuration and ongoing success at the next credit unions. You hand them something that works and a playbook to run it.

This role requires roughly 20% of travel on average, with one- to two-week stretches around kickoffs and go-lives. Based in the US, able to travel on short notice

You'll join the Agents team, a small, senior group operating as a startup inside Clutch: seven engineers, a dedicated AI product group, and AI sales specialists, working directly with some of the largest credit unions in the country. Every agent is staffed by a pair of engineers, one leaning toward the conversation and LLM side, one toward software and integrations, with a pod lead who owns the product day to day. There is no layer between you and the customer, and no layer between you and the platform team.

The operating style is direct, written, and evidence-driven: BLUF updates, evals over opinions, business outcomes over ticket counts. The team is spread across Brazil, Argentina and the US and works async by default. Being on site is a tool we use at specific moments (kickoff, live testing, go-live), not a permanent posting.

Map where the current implementation process loses time and where a default or a tool would have removed a round trip

#7270. Velocity Over Volume: Archer Roose Wines on Building Brand Loyalty in a Competitive Market

What does it take to disrupt a legacy industry without losing sight of what consumers actually want? Sometimes, the answer lies in rethinking something as familiar as the packaging.

But turning an unconventional product idea into a lasting brand requires more than innovation. It takes a distinctive voice, genuine consumer insight, and a commitment to sustainable growth.

In this episode of Adspeak by ADWEEK, chief content officer Zoë Ruderman sits down with Marian Leitner-Waldman, founder and CEO of Archer Roose Wines, and Elizabeth Banks, actor, producer, director, and chief creative officer at Archer Roose Wines, to explore how they’re challenging conventions in the wine industry.

The conversation examines how canned wine, premium positioning, and irreverent brand storytelling are helping Archer Roose rethink wine accessibility and redefine consumer perceptions of the category.

The guests discuss how identifying an overlooked consumer frustration, the waste of unfinished wine bottles, helped inspire a different approach to wine innovation.

They also explain why brand voice matters as much as product quality, and how humor, authenticity, and a willingness to avoid condescension can build meaningful consumer engagement.

A central theme is the importance of Velocity Over Volume: prioritizing reorder metrics, customer loyalty, and sustainable growth over short-lived viral success. The discussion also explores Archer Roose’s patient partnership marketing strategy with JetBlue, demonstrating how long-term thinking can turn an unexpected opportunity into a lasting distribution channel.

Marian Leitner-Waldman is the Founder and CEO of Archer Roose Wines, where she is redefining wine accessibility through premium, single-serve canned wine. Built around the insight that consumers often want just a glass rather than an entire bottle, Archer Roose has become the No. 1 canned wine by on-premise velocity. Marian has driven its growth through consumer-led innovation, strategic partnerships, and a focus on customer loyalty. Before entering the wine industry, she worked in global health finance, raising $57 million for Gavi and helping lead a $2 billion campaign for the Global Partnership for Education.

Elizabeth Banks is an award-nominated actor, producer, director, and entrepreneur known for her versatility across film and television. Her notable credits include The Hunger Games, Pitch Perfect, Spider-Man, 30 Rock, and The 40-Year-Old Virgin. She co-founded Brownstone Productions, the company behind the Pitch Perfect franchise, and has directed films including Pitch Perfect 2 and Charlie’s Angels. Beyond entertainment, Elizabeth serves as Chief Creative Officer at Archer Roose Wines, bringing her creative perspective to the brand’s marketing, storytelling, and category innovation.

You'll work directly with senior leaders to shape the GTM systems roadmap, turn business strategy into technical plans, and guide decisions that span multiple teams. You'll also help grow the engineers around you, setting the technical standards and practices the team builds on.

You understand the difference between owning a system and gatekeeping one. Sales lives in Salesforce. Marketing lives in HubSpot. Your job is to make sure both are governed well enough that others can work in them effectively, and powerful enough that the GTM team can do things they couldn't do before.

You'll thrive as a Staff GTM Systems Engineer if you:

Are known as the go-to person for tough technical problems, and have good judgment about which ones matter most.

Think in systems and long time horizons, and can make confident architectural decisions with incomplete information.

Can clearly explain complex technical concepts and tradeoffs to engineers and executives alike.

Believe systems should be managed with engineering discipline.

Ship fast and reliably — your work holds up in production.

Are a fast learner, frequently picking up new languages and tools.

Find real satisfaction in mentoring other engineers and helping them grow.

Own the architecture of the GTM systems stack: define how Salesforce, HubSpot, enrichment, sales intelligence, billing, and the data platform fit together, and write the technical strategy that guides how it evolves.

Lead build-vs-buy and procurement decisions: evaluate vendors, run proofs of concept, assess security and integration fit, and partner with Director of GTM Systems and Analytics, Finance, Legal, and IT on selection and renewals.

Shape the GTM systems roadmap with senior leaders across Sales, Marketing, RevOps, Finance, and Engineering: align on requirements, sequence investments, make clear tradeoffs, and lead cross-functional initiatives to completion.

Own Salesforce as the engineering system of record for revenue: data model, flows and automation, validation rules, field governance, and access control.

Own HubSpot for campaigns and lead generation, along with the HubSpot-Salesforce sync — so leads, contacts, and engagement signals reach the revenue pipeline without drift or silent failures.

Build and maintain integrations across the GTM stack, and partner with GTM Analytics Engineering on reliable data flows to the central data platform.

Design and deploy agentic workflows that consume GTM data, surface actionable signals, and automate manual operations — and set the patterns and guardrails others build on.

Establish observability and change management for critical GTM systems: alerts, dashboards, and runbooks; reviewed code changes, documented config, and rollback paths.

Mentor engineers across experience levels and set the technical standards for the team.

Partner with the Billing Systems Engineer to automate the CPQ-to-Billing handoff.

Extensive engineering experience in GTM or business systems, including owning a GTM stack's architecture end to end — build-vs-buy calls, vendor evaluations, and systems designed for a multi-year horizon.

Deep, hands-on Salesforce expertise — you understand the data model, have built complex flows and automation, and you think about it as an engineering system, not an admin tool. This is the primary bar.

HubSpot experience in a marketing context, including the HubSpot-Salesforce sync and what breaks and why.

Technical depth beyond the UI — you've built integrations with APIs, webhooks, and event streams, and you know when a native tool (a Flow, a Clay table, a HubSpot workflow) is the right answer versus when it's time to write code.

Agentic workflow experience — you've designed and deployed AI agents that act on GTM data in production.

Strong change management practices — you've shipped changes to live systems while protecting active deals and campaigns.

Experience influencing senior stakeholders — translating business strategy into system requirements and roadmaps.

A history of mentoring engineers, from early-career to senior.

Salesforce CPQ: approvals, order forms, discounting rules, and the closed-won-to-billing path.

SQL and data modeling fluency; familiarity with a warehouse-centric stack (BigQuery, Snowflake, dbt, reverse ETL).

Experience with billing platforms (Stripe, Zuora, Maxio) and the handoffs between GTM systems and billing.

Experience managing a GTM tooling budget or vendor portfolio.

Experience as a technical lead on a GTM or business systems team.

An opportunity to build something truly impactful to the world

Contribute to open source alongside world-class engineers

#7262. Home Depot, ESPN Among 2026 Brand Hall of Fame Inductees

The Brand Hall of Fame has inducted five new members for 2026.

The Home Depot, ESPN, Disney, Budweiser, and Chick-fil-A are the five inductees. They join the inaugural 2024 class of honorees including Coca-Cola, the NFL, Levi’s, McDonald’s, and MTV, as well as the 2025 class of Gatorade, American Express, Fender, and Dove.

The new additions, who were celebrated at the Brand Hall of Fame Awards Gala, hosted by The Gathering Festival in Banff, Canada in October, bring the total Brand Hall of Fame list to 15 companies.

Each year, five new winners are selected to the Brand Hall of Fame by a committee of senior marketers to join the Brand Hall of Fame from a list of 100 top nominees. Nominees don’t need to lead their category, but they are graded against 10 criteria to demonstrate sustained success over a long-term period.

“Every brand in this room has been tested by decades of change and is still here, still growing, still shaping how people live,” said Mandy Balak, president of The Gathering Festival and a member of the selection committee, in a statement. “That’s rare. “The Hall exists to study it as much as celebrate it, so the next generation of builders knows what it actually takes.”

Also at the Gala The One Club for Creativity honored Apple as the first brand to ever be named in its Creative Hall of Fame.

“What does it take to build a lasting creative legacy? For Apple, it’s a commitment to creativity that runs through its products, its design, its communications and the tools it puts in other people’s hands, held for decades,” said Adam Izen, chief awards officer, The One Club for Creativity. “The Creative Hall of Fame has always celebrated the people who shaped our creative world. Recognizing a brand for the first time acknowledges the role organizations play in creating the conditions for great work to happen, again and again.”

See the Brand Hall of Fame winners below:

Nominations for the 2027 Brand Hall of Fame are open until Dec. 31.

Together with the RevOps team, you own the shared language of the business (definitions, data, and processes) so commercial teams make decisions from one view of it.

We're building toward a much larger company but we aren't one yet, and most of this role has no playbook. You'll define the process, build the tool, and hand it to the team. If you want established structure, this likely isn't the role.

You'll report to the Director of Business Operations and work directly with senior leaders across the company as a neutral partner to every function rather than an advocate for one. Your work will be visible, and it will significantly influence how we make commercial decisions.

The team doesn't just own systems. They build automation, tooling, and processes, and they're close partners to Sales and Marketing. Startup experience. You've worked in a fast-moving, ever-changing environment where structure wasn't already there, and you built it. This is not a role for someone whose experience is only at large, established enterprises.

Strong analytical and financial modeling skills, including forecasting. You're very comfortable building models in spreadsheets from scratch, and they hold up to scrutiny. Data-first and self-sufficient. You open the dataset before you build the slide, you're always looking for more data to test an idea, and you're comfortable getting into raw data yourself. You use AI to analyze it faster and know enough to check its work.

Strong scripting and automation skills in a language such as Go or Python, and comfort reading infrastructure as code such as Terraform.

A pragmatic approach to risk: you can explain why a finding matters to the business and how fast it needs to be fixed.

Experience with real-time media, networking or WebRTC systems.

Experience supporting compliance audits such as HIPAA, PCI-DSS, ISO 27001 or SOC 2.

Incident response or application security experience.

Experience in AI security, such as securing LLM applications or agent infrastructure.

An opportunity to build something truly impactful to the world

Contribute to open source alongside world-class engineers

#7232. ComPsych Corporation: مستشار توجيه (Guidance Consultant)

تُعد شركة ComPsych® Corporation أكبر مزود عالمي لخدمات الصحة النفسية وبرامج GuidanceResources® للحياة. وانطلاقاً من التزامها الراسخ بالابتكار المتواصل وتبنيها لنهج شامل في تقديم الرعاية، توفر ComPsych خدماتها لأكثر من 163 مليون فرد موزعين على 200 دولة. وتحت مظلة علامتنا التجارية GuidanceResources®، نقدم برامج مخصصة ومتكاملة تماماً تشمل مجالات الصحة السلوكية، وإدارة حالات الغياب، ومسارات العافية؛ وهي برامج تهدف إلى تمكين الموظفين من عيش حياة أكثر صحة وإنتاجية، فضلاً عن تعزيز التميز المؤسسي. تفضل بزيارة موقع compsych.com لتكتشف الأسباب التي دفعت 40% من الشركات المدرجة ضمن قائمة "Fortune 500" لاختيار ComPsych لتلبية احتياجاتها المتعلقة بالصحة النفسية.

ComPsych® Corporation is the world’s largest provider of mental health services and GuidanceResources® for life. Fueled by a commitment to relentless innovation and a comprehensive approach to care, ComPsych provides services to more than 163 million individuals across 200 countries. Under our GuidanceResources® brand, our personalized and fully integrated programs include behavioral health, absence, and wellness journeys, which empower employees to lead healthier and more productive lives, while driving organizational excellence. Visit compsych.com to find out why 40% of the Fortune 500 choose ComPsych for their mental health needs.

المسمى الوظيفي هو "استشاري توجيه" (Guidance Consultant)، وهي وظيفة تعتمد نظام العمل عن بُعد ومقرها دولة الإمارات العربية المتحدة. تُعد هذه الوظيفة عقداً محدد المدة وبدوام كامل، وهي مثالية للمتخصصين في مجال الصحة النفسية ممن يقدّرون التميز السريري ويتحلون بالتعاطف في نهجهم المهني، فضلاً عن قدرتهم على النجاح والتميز في بيئة عمل سريعة الإيقاع تضع سهولة الوصول وسرعة الاستجابة والرعاية المتمحورة حول العميل في

يقوم مستشارو التوجيه بتقديم دعم مباشر للعملاء عبر الهاتف والمحادثات النصية، حيث يتناولون القضايا المتعلقة بالصحة السلوكية، والرفاه العاطفي، والشؤون الأسرية، ومسائل العافية الشاملة؛ وذلك بمنتهى الاحترافية والتعاطف، وبالاستناد إلى حكم سريري سديد.

وبصفتها جزءاً من أكبر جهة مزودة في العالم لـ "برامج مساعدة الموظفين" (EAP) وخدمات الرعاية المُدارة للصحة السلوكية، فإن هذه الوظيفة تتيح فرصة استثنائية للنمو المهني. إذ يوظف المستشارون خبراتهم السريرية لتقديم الدعم الفوري في لحظة الحاجة، وتوجيه العملاء نحو الموارد المتاحة، وتسهيل إجراء الإحالات المناسبة وفي الوقت المناسب، لضمان استمرارية الرعاية وتحقيق نتائج إيجابية.

The job title is Guidance Consultant and is a remote working role residing in the country of United Arab Emirates. This is a Fixed-Term, Full-Time position and is ideal for mental health professionals who value clinical excellence and lead with empathy, and who thrive in a fast-paced environment where access, responsiveness, and client-centered care are essential.

Guidance Consultants provide direct client support via telephone and chat, addressing behavioral health, emotional well-being, family concerns, and overall wellness-related issues with professionalism, compassion, and sound clinical judgment.

As part of the world’s largest provider of Employee Assistance Programs (EAP) and managed behavioral health care services, this role offers an exceptional opportunity for professional growth. Consultants leverage their clinical expertise to deliver in-the-moment support, guide clients through available resources, and facilitate timely, appropriate referrals to ensure continuity of care and positive outcomes.

تقديم الدعم عبر الهاتف والمحادثة النصية من خلال إجراء تقييمات سريرية أولية شاملة، وإحالة العملاء إلى الموارد المناسبة، بما في ذلك المعالجون النفسيون، والمرشدون، وموارد العافية ذات الصلة.

إجراء تقييمات للعملاء وتطبيق منهجيات التدريب (Coaching) لتطوير استراتيجيات مخصصة للصحة والعافية تدعم إحداث تغيير إيجابي في السلوك.

ضمان الالتزام ببروتوكولات القسم، وأفضل الممارسات المتبعة في القطاع، والمتطلبات الخاصة بالعملاء؛ وذلك للحفاظ على الامتثال التنظيمي وضمان تقديم خدمات عالية الجودة.

تقديم الخدمات بأسلوب يتسم بالكفاءة الثقافية، والشمولية، والاحترام للأفراد من خلفيات متنوعة وذوي احتياجات متباينة.

توظيف التقدير السليم للمواقف، والتفكير النقدي، ومهارات حل المشكلات لتقييم الحالات، ومعالجة الشواغل بسرعة، وتسهيل تقديم الدعم المناسب لتعزيز تجربة العميل الشاملة.

توعية العملاء بالموارد المتاحة، ومعايير الأهلية، والخدمات التي يقدمها البرنامج بأسلوب مهني ومتعاطف.

الحفاظ على توثيق دقيق وشامل وفي الوقت المناسب لجميع التفاعلات مع العملاء ضمن نظام قاعدة البيانات الخاص بالمؤسسة.

البقاء على اطلاع دائم بأحدث الاتجاهات والموارد وأفضل الممارسات الناشئة في مجال الصحة السلوكية، وذلك لتعزيز جودة تقديم الخدمات بشكل مستمر.

التعاون مع الأطراف المعنية (أصحاب المصلحة) داخل المؤسسة وخارجها لضمان استمرارية الرعاية ومواءمة الخدمات مع التوقعات المحددة لكل عميل.

الالتزام الصارم بالسرية الطبية ولوائح حماية البيانات المعمول بها (مثل: GDPR في أوروبا، وPDPA في سنغافورة، وPIPEDA في كندا، وPDPL في الإمارات العربية المتحدة).

يُشترط الحصول على درجة الماجستير أو ما يعادلها من مؤهلات الدراسات العليا في علم النفس، أو الخدمة الاجتماعية، أو الصحة السلوكية، أو أي مجال ذي صلة بالصحة السلوكية من مؤسسة معترف بها.

في حال كان الإطار التنظيمي المهني المعمول به في بلد أو ولاية العمل يعترف بشهادة البكالوريوس كمعيار أساسي للالتحاق بالتخصص ذي الصلة، يُمكن اعتبار هذه الشهادة من مؤسسة معترف بها، بالإضافة إلى الخبرة العملية ذات الصلة بعد الحصول عليها، معادلةً لها.

يجب على المرشحين استيفاء جميع المتطلبات التعليمية والخبرة اللازمة لممارسة المهنة و/أو الحصول على الترخيص أو التسجيل لدى الهيئة التنظيمية المختصة في ولاية عملهم، حيثما يكون هذا التسجيل أو الترخيص مطلوبًا بموجب القانون أو اللوائح.

يجب على المرشحين استيفاء جميع المتطلبات التعليمية والخبرة اللازمة للتأهل للممارسة المهنية و/أو الترخيص أو التسجيل لدى الهيئة التنظيمية المختصة في ولاية عملهم، حيثم

يكون هذا التسجيل أو الترخيص مطلوبًا بموجب القانون أو اللوائح.

يُفضل الخبرة في العمل ضمن بيئات متعددة الثقافات أو دولية

القدرة على تقديم دعم يركز على العميل مع تعاطف قوي، ومهارات استماع فعّالة، ومهارات تدريبية

قدرة عالية على التقييم السريري وإدارة المواقف عالية الخطورة أو الأزمات

مهارات تواصل ممتازة مع توجه قوي نحو خدمة العملاء

القدرة على التفكير النقدي وإدارة مهام متعددة في بيئات عمل سريعة الوتيرة

مستوى عالٍ من الاحترافية والنزاهة والحساسية الثقافية

القدرة على التعامل مع المعلومات الحساسة بسرية تامة والحفاظ على خصوصيتها

سرعة البديهة، والنشاط، والإيجابية، والاحترافية، مع مهارات مثبتة في إدارة المهام المتعددة والتفكير النقدي وإدارة الأزمات أو المواقف عالية الخطورة

يُشترط توفر اتصال إنترنت عالي السرعة ومستقر وموثوق، بالإضافة إلى مساحة عمل خالية من المشتتات أو المقاطعات أو الضوضاء.

القدرة على الظهور عبر الكاميرا أثناء التدريبات والاجتماعات والفعاليات المتعلقة بالعمل.

يجب أن تكون قادراً على الجلوس أو الوقوف أمام المكتب لفترات طويلة أثناء العمل على جهاز الحاسوب.

يتراوح نطاق الأجر لهذا المنصب ما بين 110,000 و128,560 درهماً إماراتياً سنوياً. وسيتم تحديد قيمة التعويضات وفقاً لمعدلات السوق المحلية السائدة والمتطلبات القانونية المعمول بها. وفي الحالات التي يقتضيها القانون الساري، سيتم تزويد المرشحين بمعلومات نطاق الراتب خلال عملية التوظيف أو بناءً على طلبهم.

تُقدَّم المزايا وفقاً لقانون العمل الإماراتي، وتشمل الإجازة السنوية، والعطلات الرسمية، والتأمين الصحي الإلزامي، ومكافأة نهاية الخدمة. وسيتم تقديم تفاصيل إضافية خلال مرحلة تقديم العرض.

نحن جهة توظيف تكفل تكافؤ الفرص، ونلتزم بممارسات توظيف عادلة وشفافة وقائمة على الجدارة. سيتم النظر في طلبات التوظيف المقدمة من جميع المتقدمين المؤهلين دون أي تمييز يستند إلى العمر، أو الجنس، أو الهوية الجندرية أو التعبير عنها، أو التوجه الجنسي، أو العرق، أو اللون، أو الأصل الإثني، أو الجنسية، أو الدين أو المعتقد، أو الإعاقة، أو حالة الحمل أو الوضع الأبوي، أو الحالة الاجتماعية أو الأسرية، أو الوضع العسكري أو وضع المحاربين القدامى، أو الخلفية الاجتماعية والاقتصادية، أو المعلومات الجينية، أو أي خصائص أخرى يحميها القانون المحلي أو الوطني أو الدولي المعمول به.

نحن ملتزمون بتوفير عملية توظيف شاملة وميسرة للجميع. إذا كنت بحاجة إلى أي تسهيلات أو تعديلات في أي مرحلة من مراحل عملية التوظيف بسبب إعاقة أو لأي سبب آخر، يرجى التواصل معنا عبر البريد الإلكتروني [email protected]، وسنعمل معك لتلبية احتياجاتك.

تلتزم شركة ComPsych Corporation وشركاتها التابعة بالتعامل مع بياناتك الشخصية بمسؤولية وشفافية. سيتم استخدام المعلومات التي يتم جمعها أثناء عملية التوظيف لتقييم طلبك، وحيثما ينطبق ذلك، لإعداد وإدارة عرض التوظيف المقدم إليك. ستتم معالجة بياناتك الشخصية وفقاً لقوانين الخصوصية المعمول بها في بلد إقامتك أو نطاق ولايتك القضائية، والتي قد تشمل قانون خصوصية المستهلك في كاليفورنيا (CCPA)، واللائحة العامة لحماية البيانات في الاتحاد الأوروبي (GDPR)، وقانون حماية المعلومات الشخصية والوثائق الإلكترونية في كندا (PIPEDA) والتشريعات الإقليمية المتعلقة بالخصوصية، وغيرها من قوانين الخصوصية الوطنية أو المحلية السارية. للحصول على تفاصيل كاملة حول البيانات الشخصية التي نجمعها، وكيفية استخدامنا لها، وحقوقك بصفتك متقدماً للوظيفة، وكيفية التواصل معنا لطرح أسئلة تتعلق بالخصوصية، يرجى مراجعة إشعارات خصوصية بيانات المتقدمين ذات الصلة: "إشعار حماية البيانات للمتقدمين للوظائف في المنطقة الاقتصادية الأوروبية وسويسرا والمملكة المتحدة" و"إشعار خصوصية المتقدمين للوظائف على المستوى العالمي".

Understand each customer’s architecture, use cases, constraints, and goals.

Align Supabase capabilities with the customer’s roadmap.

Act as the technical owner for your assigned accounts, documenting environments, mapping dependencies, and tracking technical risks.

Help customers reach early success milestones to shorten time to value and reduce early friction.

Regularly review customer environments and usage patterns to identify scalability, performance, reliability, security, and compliance risks and opportunities.

Plan upgrades and lifecycle events, and run architecture reviews for upcoming initiatives, including capacity and growth planning, feature readiness, and early identification of technical blockers.

Coordinate across Support and Engineering on complex or high-impact issues. TAMs are not frontline support, but provide the context, prioritization, and follow-through that drive resolution, root cause analysis, and preventative action.

A proactive mindset: you spot risks and optimization opportunities before the customer does, and you treat your accounts like a personal portfolio.

Sound escalation judgment: you know when to escalate, when to resolve internally, and how to communicate clearly under pressure without creating noise.

Very strong communication skills. TAMs explain complex issues to non-technical executives and root causes to senior engineers, often in the same day.

Awareness of how TAM performance shows up in retention and NRR, including surfacing expansion signals and renewal risks early.

Experience working in async or globally distributed teams.

We're also embracing AI thoughtfully — both in how we build and how we hire to ensure our team is adaptable, innovative, and ready for what's next.

Most Kit creators don't have a dedicated Creator Growth Manager, and when their emails start landing in spam, they need someone who can find the cause quickly and explain it in plain language. We're looking for a Deliverability and Compliance Specialist to own deliverability support for that long tail of creators, run Kit's abuse desk, and make the compliance calls that protect the sending reputation every creator on Kit shares.

You'll also build the playbooks, training, and AI-assisted workflows that help Support and contractors handle more deliverability questions on their own, and you'll partner with our infrastructure engineers as we run more of our sending in-house. A creator can't build a lasting business if their emails don't arrive, so this work connects directly to our mission.

This is a full-time individual contributor role for someone who likes digging through headers and bounce logs to find the real cause of a problem, can explain DMARC alignment to a first-time creator without jargon, and is comfortable making a firm call when a situation is gray. You don't wait for someone to tell you what needs fixing. You proactively spot opportunities, identify patterns, and take the initiative to improve deliverability before problems arise.

You'll report to Alyssa Dulin, Director of Creator Success, and collaborate closely with:

Our Deliverability team: a Deliverability Lead who supports our highest-value accounts and leads deliverability education, and a Deliverability Specialist who onboards and ramps up large senders moving to Kit

Our infrastructure engineers, who run Kit's sending infrastructure

Our compliance and anti-abuse engineers, who build the automated checks that keep spammers off Kit

Product Specialists and the Support team, who handle the front line and escalate deliverability issues to you

Customer Success Managers (we call them Creator Growth Managers at Kit), Sales and Migrations, and Product Managers

First Week: Get set up in Kit, our admin tools, and our support and ticketing systems, and meet our distributed team in Get To Know You chats (GTKYs). Meet the deliverability team and get an introduction on the current projects. Read our deliverability playbooks and SOPs, and get access to the monitoring tools you'll use every day: Google Postmaster Tools, Microsoft SNDS, blocklist monitoring, and our internal delivery dashboards.

First Month: Shadow the Deliverability team and Product Specialists on deliverability tickets, account approvals, and abuse reports, then start answering queue tickets with review. Join the daily monitoring rotation (blocklist delisting, Postmaster and delivery monitoring) and the weekly Deliverability team meeting. Learn how account approvals, compliance flags, and our in-house sending platform work. By the end of the month, work deliverability tickets and abuse reports on your own and send your first delisting requests. Project: build a recurring report showing the trending deliverability ticket reasons so we understand what’s causing how to reduce friction for customers and how to solve it

First Six Months: Own the deliverability support queue for creators without a dedicated CGM: spam-folder placement, bounces, authentication and DMARC setup, and escalations from Product Specialists. Kit is growing, so we're looking for a Senior Analytics Engineer to own Kit's sales and commerce data from end to end.

Accurate sales data gives our team a clear picture of the pipeline and the confidence to make informed decisions. That’s what makes this role so important.

Your primary focus will be Sales: the dbt models that turn our CRM data into trusted pipeline, deal, and funnel metrics, and the Omni dashboards our sales leader and Senior Revenue Operations Manager use to run the business. You'll also model creator commerce data, so we understand what creators sell and earn through Kit, and you'll pick up reporting work with our Product team, such as feature usage reporting. On a small data team, we step outside our usual lanes when the need is there, so the mix will shift over time.

You'll build for two kinds of consumers: people and AI. Teammates increasingly answer their own questions by querying our semantic layer through Omni's AI and MCP connections to AI tools. That means your models, metric definitions, and documentation need to be clear and correct enough for an AI agent to use them without a data teammate in the loop.

This is a full-time individual contributor role for someone who likes being close to the business, enjoys turning messy operational data into numbers people trust, and is happy to work on both the models and the dashboards that sit on top of them. When Sales and Product have clear, reliable data, we help more creators build more valuable businesses with Kit.

You'll report to Sam Umachi, Head of Data (Linkedin).

Sales and RevOps are your primary partners, who will rely on the models and reporting you own. You'll also partner with our Product team on reporting for features like Visual Automations and on how creators use Kit.

Within our data team, you'll work alongside a Staff Analytics Engineer who owns org-wide semantic layer governance and modeling standards, a Senior Data Engineer who owns ingestion and pipelines, and a Senior Business Analytics Partner focused on revenue opportunities. This role has no direct reports.

Get set up in our core tools: dbt Cloud, GitHub, Amazon Redshift, Omni, HubSpot, Linear, Hightouch and Slack. Set up your AI-assisted development environment and learn the shared AI workflows our data team uses. Open and merge your first small pull request so you learn our review and deployment flow early.

First Month: Map how HubSpot objects (deals, pipelines and stages, companies, contacts, and owners) flow into our dbt models, and document the gaps. Take over day-to-day ownership of the sales models and Omni dashboards that teammates have been covering. Audit current sales reporting and list where definitions differ or numbers don't reconcile. Get familiar with how commerce data (products creators sell and the revenue they earn through Kit) lands in the warehouse today. Agree with RevOps and Sales on the most important questions they need answered. Review teammates' pull requests and contribute to our shared SQL, dbt, and documentation standards. Share a written reflection with the team on what you've learned and where you'd focus first.

First Six Months: Ship and maintain well-tested, documented dbt models for core sales metrics, such as pipeline, deal velocity, stage conversion, win rates, and the sales-led funnel. Expose those metrics through the Omni semantic layer, working within the team's governance standards. Rebuild or refresh the core sales dashboards so pipeline reviews and forecasting conversations run on numbers that don't need manual reconciliation. Resolve competing sales metric definitions by writing down the options and tradeoffs, aligning with Sales and RevOps, and documenting the outcome. Model creator-level commerce data, such as products sold, revenue earned through Kit, and conversion, in partnership with data engineering. Write semantic layer descriptions, metric definitions, and documentation that let Omni's AI and MCP-connected AI tools answer routine sales questions correctly, and test them against real questions from Sales, RevOps and Product. Get Sales to the point where routine questions are answered through self-serve, whether in Omni dashboards, Omni's AI, or AI tools connected over MCP, rather than one-off requests.

First Year: Be the recognized owner of Kit's sales data, the person Sales and RevOps go to first and trust without second-guessing. Give our revenue analysis work the sales and commerce models it depends on, so deeper questions about pipeline health and creator earnings can be answered quickly, thereby enabling us to make creators more successful. Leave your domains well documented and well tested, legible to new teammates, stakeholders, and the AI agents that query our semantic layer. Help raise the team's modeling quality through code review, pairing, and sharing what you learn.

Deep dbt experience. You build modular, tested, documented dbt projects, and you're comfortable with incremental models, snapshots, macros, CI, and scheduled jobs in dbt Cloud or similar.

Advanced SQL. You reason carefully about grain, joins, and window functions, and you write queries that are readable and performant on a cloud warehouse.

BI and semantic layer experience. You can build dashboards and semantic models in Omni, Looker, or a similar BI tool, and you design metric definitions that people and AI tools can both query correctly.

Visionary in 2025 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for Business Orchestration and Automation Technologies. ranked 3rd in Flexa's 2026 Most Flexible Companies, We’re growing fast and looking for top talent to join our team. If you want meaningful work, visible impact and put something genuinely rare on your CV, keep reading.

We are hiring a Sr. Manager, Total Rewards to lead the design, delivery, and continuous improvement of Camunda’s global compensation, benefits, and equity programs. This person will partner closely with the People team, Finance, and Legal to make sure our rewards are competitive, equitable, compliant, and easy to understand in every country where we operate. This is a hands-on, strategic role for a rewards leader who wants to build and run programs as a trusted business partner, not just administer them. The right candidate will bring deep technical rewards expertise, strong AI and analytical skills, and a passion for a great employee experience. We also expect this leader to be AI-forward in how we approach compensation strategy and job leveling, not only in how they use AI to get their own work done. That means using AI and data-driven methods to shape pay philosophy, benchmark and model market data, design and calibrate our job architecture and leveling framework, and make faster, more consistent, and more transparent rewards decisions.

Own the global Total Rewards strategy across compensation, benefits, equity, and recognition, and make sure it helps Camunda attract, retain, and motivate top talent. Set an AI-forward vision for how Camunda designs and runs compensation, and turn it into a practical roadmap.

Lead the annual compensation cycle, including merit, promotion, and equity planning, in partnership with People Business Partners, Finance, and business leaders.

Own job architecture, leveling, and grading frameworks, and keep the Job Catalog accurate as the company grows. Apply AI and data-driven approaches to job leveling, such as consistent role-to-level mapping, calibration, and level-drift detection, with human oversight.

Run market benchmarking and compensation analysis, and turn the findings into clear, data-driven recommendations for leadership. Use AI to speed up and improve benchmarking, pay modeling, and scenario analysis.

Manage benefits programs and vendor relationships across regions, including EOR providers such as Remote.com, brokers, and equity administration platforms, with a clear view of cost, service levels, and employee value.

Ensure compliance with pay transparency, pay equity, and local compensation and benefits regulations in every jurisdiction where Camunda operates.

Partner with Finance on compensation budgeting and equity pool management, and track total rewards spend against a clear baseline.

Advise People Business Partners and hiring managers on offers, pay decisions, and exceptions, balancing internal equity, market data, budget and capturing and retaining strong AI talent..

Identify and adopt AI and automation tools that improve rewards analytics, benchmarking, and employee communications, and ensure AI use complies with local laws and regulations.

Lead, coach, and develop the Total Rewards team, setting clear priorities and growth paths.

Manage Camunda’s Relationship with our EOR.

Ability and/or willingness to use our product.

Bachelor’s degree in HR, Finance, Business, or a related field. A CCP, CEBS, or similar certification is a plus.

8+ years of Total Rewards experience, including significant compensation and benefits experience at a global, multi-country technology or SaaS company.

Hands-on experience leading a global compensation cycle (merit, promotion, and equity) and running market benchmarking with tools such as Radford, Pave, or similar.

Experience managing benefits, EOR, and equity vendor relationships, including platforms such as Remote or similar.

Strong analytical and modeling skills, with the ability to translate complex data into practical, business-pragmatic recommendations.

Working knowledge of pay transparency, pay equity, and compensation regulations across multiple jurisdictions, ideally including the EU and USA.

Experience using AI and automation tools to improve rewards analytics and workflows.

AI-forward approach to compensation strategy and job leveling, with real examples of using AI or advanced analytics to shape pay philosophy, model market data, build or calibrate job architecture and levels, or improve pay decisions, and the judgment to keep humans accountable for fairness, compliance, and explainability.

Excellent written and verbal communication, with the ability to build trust across People, Finance, and Legal.

Experience leading or developing a team and influencing senior stakeholders.

We offer competitive, fair, and transparent compensation. Salary ranges are location-based, with Standard and Major markets (global tech hubs) reflecting local competition.

The Annual Total Target Cash (base salary + 100% variable target, where applicable) shown below spans from the minimum in a Standard market to the maximum in a Major market. Final offers depend on skills, experience, and location, and we typically hire in the first half of the range to allow room for growth:

United States: $183,000.00 to $295,100.00

United Kingdom: £114,900.00 to £189,000.00

If you’re based elsewhere, you’ll be hired via Remote.com (our global employer partner), and your Talent Acquisition Partner will provide a personalized Total Rewards Calculator after your first interview.

Equity: We also offer equity (where applicable) through our Virtual Stock Option Plan (VSOP).

We invest in your wellbeing, growth, and ability to connect, along with perks that support you no matter where you’re based. Our benefits are globally designed and locally delivered where applicable.

Remote & Flexible: Work from anywhere with the setup that suits you, home office budget, co-working space support, and flexible time off to recharge when you need it.

In Person Connection: We invest in meaningful face time through our Annual Kickoff (Vienna in 2025, Madrid in 2026!), team offsites, and Camundi Connection Budgets, including contributing to meetups while travelling,, and local gatherings with fellow Camundi.

Health & Wellbeing: Access locally tailored healthcare, Modern Health for global mental wellbeing, and our Live Well Lifestyle Spending Account (LSA), a flexible, global benefit that puts you in control of your whole life, not just work, from: staying active, to caring for family, exploring personal passions, meaningful experiences, and investing in your financial wellbeing. The Live Well program launches in 2026 and scales to €1,000 annually from 2027.

Financial Security: Retirement and pension plans (often with company contributions), plus life and disability insurance where relevant.

Professional Growth: Up to $/€/£1,000 per year for self-driven learning: courses, certifications, books, you decide!

AI in our hiring process: Camunda may use AI tools to aid the screening of applications and during the interview process. You can learn more here

All qualified applicants will receive consideration without regard to gender, race, ethnicity, religion, belief, sexual orientation, age, disability or any other protected characteristics under applicable law. Please be aware that scammers may impersonate Camunda by reaching out about job opportunities. Camunda will only contact candidates from an email address ending in @camunda.com or @talent.camunda.com. We will never ask candidates for bank account details, checks, payment, or other sensitive financial information as part of our hiring process. If you receive a suspicious message, please do not respond, click any links, or share personal information.

By opting in to SMS use, please be aware that:

Standard text message and data rates may apply; and

By opting in, you will be enrolled directly in ongoing recruiting text messages from Camunda.

To opt-out, simply select 'No' when prompted during your application. You can also change your preferences at any time by contacting talent-operations@camunda.com.
